The Service Company had[2K⋯ thirty[2K⋯ -one days'[2K⋯ actual notice before the term ended —[2K⋯ during which it could[2K⋯ seek[2K⋯ replacement[2K⋯ work — and it[2K⋯ continued to perform and be[2K⋯ paid through[2K⋯ December 31 under[2K⋯ the contract it says[2K⋯ was renewed. Unless[2K⋯ it can point to specific reliance[2K⋯ ([2K⋯ e.g., work[2K⋯ it turned[2K⋯ away for[2K⋯ 1991[2K⋯ because it believed the[2K⋯ contract had renewed[2K⋯ ), its[2K⋯ prejudice is speculative.[2K⋯ On this record the[2K⋯ late[2K⋯ notice should[2K⋯ be held[2K⋯ effective and[2K⋯ the contract treated[2K⋯ as terminated December[2K⋯ 31, 1990;[2K⋯ enforcing[2K⋯ the automatic[2K⋯ renewal[2K⋯ instead[2K⋯ would work[2K⋯ a five-year forfeiture[2K⋯ to[2K⋯ penal[2K⋯ ize a[2K⋯ 58[2K⋯ -day delay — a[2K⋯ result Pennsylvania[2K⋯ 's prejudice[2K⋯ -based[2K⋯ rule is[2K⋯ designed to avoid[2K⋯ .  3. **The[2K⋯ withheld payments ([2K⋯ October–December 1990).[2K⋯ ** Ter[2K⋯ mination of the contract[2K⋯ does not extingu[2K⋯ ish the price[2K⋯ of[2K⋯ services already rendered[2K⋯ . Construction. The clause gives each[2K⋯ prior written notice." Read literally, timely notice is the prescribed method of exercising the [2K⋯ the power[2K⋯ to terminate[2K⋯ . The[2K⋯ rationale is that[2K⋯ the notice period[2K⋯ exists to give[2K⋯ the other party lead[2K⋯ time to prepare for the[2K⋯ change[2K⋯ ([2K⋯ here[2K⋯ , to wind[2K⋯ down the[2K⋯ maintenance[2K⋯ schedule[2K⋯ and seek replacement business), not to spring[2K⋯ a forfeiture[2K⋯ ;[2K⋯ and[2K⋯ a party to an[2K⋯ automatic-renewal contract[2K⋯ always knows the expiration[2K⋯ date from[2K⋯ the contract itself[2K⋯ , so a shorter[2K⋯ notice period rarely[2K⋯ causes real[2K⋯ harm.   ▐ aura  Done. ANSWER.md is written and verified on disk.

**The analysis, in brief:** The clause presents four issues — the legal character of the 90-day notice requirement, the effect of the Hotel Owner's 58-days-late notice, the burden of proving prejudice, and the two collateral claims (withheld Oct–Dec 1990 payments; the alleged oral 20% discount). Because the clause contains no "time is of the essence" language, Pennsylvania doctrine construes it as a flexible notice provision, not a strict condition: the late notice was effective unless the Service Company demonstrates concrete prejudice from receiving 31 rather than 90 days' lead time — a burden it likely cannot meet for a maintenance contract whose expiration date was always known. So the contract terminated December 31, 1990 and the renewal-term claim fails; the Service Company may still recover the October–December price less proven service deficiencies (substantial performance); and the oral discount counterclaim fails (parol evidence rule, and it was an inducement to a renewal the owner rejected).

**One limitation to flag:** web verification of the underlying case was blocked — the project's scope allow-list refused both crawl targets (duckduckgo.com, law.justia.com). The analysis rests on the record in case-06.md and settled doctrine; if you want the underlying case confirmed by name, allow those domains (`/scope add duckduckgo.com`, `/scope add law.justia.com`) and I'll verify.
